# Bug log

Every bug the team finds — in testing, code review, a demo or production, backend or frontend — gets one row
here. The test report takes its defect list from this table: copy the table into Excel or Word as it is, and
count by Severity and Status there.

## How to log a bug

1. Add a row at the bottom with the next id (`BUG-001`, `BUG-002`, …). Never reuse or renumber an id.
2. Fill in what you know now: where, how bad, how to reproduce, expected vs. actual. Add the Jira key if the
   bug has an issue. Log it even if nobody fixes it yet.
3. Say clearly whose it is. **Found by** is who found it; **Owner** is who fixes it — normally the developer
   who owns that feature or module (ask the lead if unsure). Write people by full name, as in Jira — never
   "me", "team" or "BE". If the owner changes, update the column.
4. The owner fixes it test-first: a test that fails before the fix, named with the bug id
   (`keepsFirstImport_BUG_004`, or `it('keeps the first import (BUG-004)')` in the frontend).
5. The fix PR sets Status to `Fixed` and fills in Fixed in and Test, in the same PR. The person who found it
   (or the tester) sets `Closed` after re-checking it.
6. Keep every bug row, including `Fixed` and `Closed`, for the user's report. Never delete a row or its
   reproduction evidence when fixing the bug; append verification results and update its status instead.

**Severity:** `Critical` crash, data loss, security hole, or blocks a demo · `High` a main flow is wrong, no
workaround · `Medium` wrong, but there is a workaround · `Low` cosmetic, typo.

**Status:** `Open` → `In progress` → `Fixed` → `Closed`, or `Won't fix` (give the reason in Notes).

## Bugs

> **Id gaps (checked 2026-11-21 with `git log -S`):** there are no rows BUG-023, BUG-024, BUG-038, BUG-043 to BUG-056, BUG-118, BUG-119, BUG-128, BUG-165 and BUG-214. None was deleted from this file: `git log -S` finds no commit that added and removed them, except BUG-165. These ids were taken on parallel branches and renumbered before they reached `main`. Examples: BUG-023/024 (identity Task 7, KAN-229) were never logged; the Task 13 BUG-038 became BUG-067 (KAN-111); the BUG-022 of search Task 1 notes that BUG-023 to BUG-056 were taken on open branches; the worker BUG-214 of KAN-162 is BUG-215 to BUG-217 (note below). For 118, 119 and 128, no commit on any branch mentions the id. BUG-165 was given twice on 07/11: the identity sign-in row was renumbered BUG-178 in PR #164 (KAN-108), and the chatbot rows of KAN-179 also became BUG-178/179. Their tests still carry `_BUG_165`.
>
> **Duplicate ids:** BUG-178 and BUG-179 each have two rows (chatbot KAN-179 and identity / CI KAN-289, KAN-6). They are kept as they are, not renumbered; each row has a note. Cite them with their Jira key.
>
> **Reserved ids (2026-11-22):** BUG-366 to BUG-369 belong to the NFR run of KAN-378 (branch `test/KAN-378-…`); their rows arrive when that branch is merged.
>
> **Detected at** (added 2026-11-21): the test level that found the bug — `Review` (code review, evaluation), `UT` (unit tests: JUnit / Vitest), `IT` (integration tests: Testcontainers, `mvn verify`), `ST` (system test on staging), `AT` (acceptance test), `Local` (manual run on a local stack, the Jira dev site or tooling). It was filled from Found by, Steps and Notes.

> Integration note (KAN-243): Main keeps BUG-322 to BUG-325. The KAN-243 chatbot bugs were renumbered BUG-326 to BUG-331 to preserve unique ids.

> Integration note (KAN-162/KAN-333): Main keeps BUG-212 and BUG-213. The KAN-162 outbox bugs were renumbered BUG-215, BUG-216 and BUG-217 to preserve unique ids.

| BUG-090 | 2026-11-01 | Trần Trung Hiếu | UT | Trần Trung Hiếu | frontend · my-profile tests | Low | MySolutionsSection test edge 63 is flaky under load: its wait for SUP-140 runs a role query on every DOM change | In `frontend/`, run `npx vitest run src/features/my-profile` twice and `src/features/review` once, in parallel · expected: edge 63 passes on every run · actual: fails in 2 of 12 runs (and once in the FE Lead's full run, 1267/1269) at `waitFor(… bodyRows(table)[0] … getByText('SUP-140'))` after 1 s, with the empty page-4 table still shown. Root cause (test-only): the waitFor callback calls `getAllByRole('row')`, which waitFor re-runs on every DOM change; each run blocks the thread, so React and MSW cannot finish the page-3 render it is waiting for. Measured under the same load: 10–14 callback runs took 1.1–1.5 s of a 1.1–1.6 s wait, so the 1 s default was always nearly used up. Not the BUG-088 cause: the table element stays the same and connected | Fixed | KAN-238 | KAN-238 (flaky-tests fix) | `MySolutionsSection.test.tsx` `edge 63 moves ?page=4 to the last page with history replace, and an empty list to page 1 with MSG04 (BUG-090)` | Fixed without a longer timeout: wait with `table.findByText('SUP-140')` (a cheap text query), then check the first body row once. Same load after the fix: the wait takes 170–290 ms; 18/18 my-profile runs pass |
| BUG-091 | 2026-11-01 | Trần Trung Hiếu | UT | Trần Trung Hiếu | frontend · review Ticket Detail tests | Low | ReviewTicketDetail test AC-006.5 is flaky under load: `findByRole('link')` blocks the render it waits for | In `frontend/`, run `src/features/review` with two `src/features/my-profile` runs in parallel · expected: AC-006.5 passes on every run · actual: fails in 4 of 6 runs (and once in the FE Lead's full run) with `Unable to find role="link" and name "<script>file0</script>"` after 1–2 s. Root cause (test-only): the first wait after render is `findByRole('link', { name })`; one link role query costs ~140 ms alone and ~200 ms under load (15 links in the app shell), and waitFor re-runs it on every DOM change while the page renders. Measured under load: 7–8 runs took 0.9–1.6 s of a 1.2–2.0 s wait; the Evidence itself was in the DOM after 0.9–1.5 s only because the queries held up the render | Fixed | KAN-238 | KAN-238 (flaky-tests fix) | `ReviewTicketDetail.test.tsx` `AC-006.5 renders filenames as text, readable sizes, and only safe references (BUG-091)` | Fixed without a longer timeout: wait with `findByText('<script>file0</script>')`, then check the link with one `getByRole`. Same load after the fix: the wait takes 300–450 ms; AC-006.5 passed 6/6 review runs. Same pattern may affect other tests whose first wait is a costly role query: BUG-092 |
| BUG-092 | 2026-11-01 | Trần Trung Hiếu | UT | Phạm Tùng Dương | frontend · review Ticket Detail tests | Low | ReviewTicketDetail test AC-006.3 AC-005.5 (BUG-089) is flaky under load: the first Approve button is not found in 1 s | In `frontend/`, run `src/features/review` with two `src/features/my-profile` runs in parallel · expected: the test passes on every run · actual: fails in 2 of 6 runs before the BUG-090/091 fix and 1 of 6 after it, `Unable to find role="button" and name "Approve"` at line 20 (2.4–3.4 s). It is the file's first test, so its first `findByRole('button', …)` with the default 1 s also loads the lazy page module (guideline 10 §6 asks for 5 s). Root cause (test-only, measured): the first wait after render only had the default 1 s, but under load the lazy page module plus the first render take longer than that; a cheap `findByText` with the same 1 s also failed (2 of 2 runs), so it is not the role query alone as in BUG-091. Baseline under the same load: 6 of 6 runs failed (test took 2.6–3.1 s) | Fixed | KAN-238 | KAN-238 (flaky-tests fix) | `ReviewTicketDetail.test.tsx` `AC-006.3 AC-005.5 keeps pending actions mounted and disabled during refetch (BUG-089)` | Fixed as guideline 10 §6 prescribes: the first wait is `findByText('Pending review', {}, { timeout: 5000 })` (cheap text query, 5 s because it loads the lazy module), then one `getByRole` per button. The cause is shown to be only load, so the 5 s is the documented first-findBy timeout, not a blanket raise. Same load after the fix: 12 of 12 runs pass (22/22 tests). Under the same 3× load, ReviewQueuePage tests also failed in the first runs (BUG-087 among them) |

| BUG-199 | 2026-11-11 | Trần Trung Hiếu | UT | Phạm Tùng Dương | backend · review `ReviewQueueEventServiceImplTest` | Low | `drainRecoversAfterAnUnexpectedError_BUG_173` is flaky in CI: it times out waiting for the healthy stream's notification | Run the backend unit tests in CI (failed on main run 42693053567, chore/KAN-305 run 42684175463, feat/KAN-182 run 42644403281 and PR #199 run 43060451940, which passed on rerun) · expected: the test passes on every run · actual: `Expecting value to be true but was false` at `awaitUntil` (`ReviewQueueEventServiceImplTest.java:282`, called at `:229`) after the 10.03 s timeout. Not reproduced locally (30/30 pass on 12 cores). Likely cause: the fake gives every listener thread one shared notification queue, but real Postgres sends each NOTIFY to every listening connection; the closed first Listener can still take the PROJECT notification within its 50 ms poll and send it to an empty subscriber list (`ReviewQueueEventServiceImpl.java:344-346`, `:591`), so `healthy` never gets it | Fixed | KAN-379 | #306 (049584a0) | ReviewQueueEventServiceImplTest.drainRecoversAfterAnUnexpectedError_BUG_173 | Not fixed; logged on Hiếu's decision (2026-11-11). Proposed fix (test only): one mock Connection/PGConnection per `getConnection()`, each NOTIFY sent to every open queue; or wait until the healthy stream is subscribed on a live listener before adding the notification. The `BUG_173` in the test name is the earlier stream bug (BUG-173 row), not this bug. Owner to be confirmed by the lead (review module, KAN-154 area). No Jira issue yet. 2026-11-17: failed again on PR #265 run 44035607155 attempt 1 (same `awaitUntil` timeout at `:282` from `:229`, 1 of 2769 unit tests); the PR does not touch the review module 2026-11-22: failed again on main run 44803313736. Fixed (test only, Anh Tran Hai): the fake keeps one notification queue per LISTENing thread and delivers each NOTIFY to all of them, as PostgreSQL does. |

Local workaround (demo kit): export `SPRING_MAIL_*` and `SMTP_*` before `mvn spring-boot:run`, `SMTP_READ_TIMEOUT_MS=15000`, `SMTP_WRITE_TIMEOUT_MS=15000`, `TSOLVE_WORKER_EMAIL_BATCH_SIZE=5` (keeps the lease check). Suggested fix: map the `SPRING_MAIL_*` placeholders in `application.yml` (`host: ${SPRING_MAIL_HOST:}`) and log the exception class on a failed send |

| BUG-324 | 2026-11-17 | bluebearccc (local demo, via Claude Code) | Local | Phạm Tùng Dương | backend · worker mock `MockWorkerTenantService` / wiki index enqueue | High | Under `worker-mock`, a published Wiki document is never indexed: `wiki-index` cannot be enqueued from the upload request | Local demo (`dev,worker,worker-mock`, `TSOLVE_STUB_WORKSPACE_ID` set, `TSOLVE_MOCK_ACTIVE_WORKSPACES` with two ids); publish a PDF in a Space · expected: a `wiki-index` job runs and `index_status` leaves `PENDING`, so Search and the Chatbot find the document · actual: the upload succeeds but the log shows `Could not enqueue wiki-index for document …` with `IllegalStateException: Enqueue requires a bound workspace` at `TenantJobServiceImpl.enqueue` (`WikiUploadServiceImpl.afterCommit`); `index_status` stays `PENDING` | Fixed | KAN-167 | PR #279 | `CatalogWorkerTenantServiceTest.boundActiveWorkspaceOnAnyThread_AC_002_1_BUG_324`, `TenantJobServiceImplTest.enqueueFromRequestBindsTheEnqueueWorkspace_BUG_324` | `MockWorkerTenantService.enqueueWorkspaceId()` (the one rule for "which Workspace may an unbound thread of a request enqueue in") knew only its own ThreadLocal and the single-active-Workspace rule; the platform `TenantContext` (stub) was not consulted, and the wiki after-commit listeners did not ask it at all. Fix: the mock's `enqueueWorkspaceId()` also takes the `TenantContext` Workspace, only on a request thread and only when it is an Active mock Workspace; the wiki listeners call `TenantJobService.enqueueFromRequest`, which binds that Workspace with `runIn` (same pattern as `ReviewNotificationEnqueuer`). `enqueue` itself still refuses an unbound thread (AC-002.2). The upload is not failed on purpose (BUG-295), and there is no re-index sweep: documents published before the fix stay `PENDING` until re-enqueued by hand |
